Similar relationship between the PFS outcomes and the AUC metric was identified. Overall, these results indicate a clear correlation between the level of exposure on resminostat and the clinical benefit in terms of PFS.

An exposure/response analysis to assess potential cardiovascular toxicities was performed. Please refer to the safety clinical part.

## Pharmacokinetics-Pharmacodynamics (PK/PD)

A PKPD model was developed based on PK and HDAC inhibition data of resminostat acquired during the FIM 7341/EM-001 (ascending multiple resminostat doses from 100 to 800 mg) in patients with advanced malignancies.

The PK data were described using a one-compartment model with zero order input (inconsistent with the 2compartment model identified in the final population analysis). Furthermore, the FIM study was supported by an initial capsule formulation (not the proposed commercial tablet with increased BA of around 40% for Cmax).

The HDAC inhibition, measured ex vivo in peripheral blood mononuclear cells (PBMC), was best described by a Receptor on/off model able to capture the shifts of PD response maxima. The derived model-based apparent KD value was 454 ± 168 nM overall consistent to measured inhibition values (570 ± 260 nM). Maximum HDAC inhibition occurred within 2h of start of dosing (not well captured by the model). Overall, model-based simulations of PD response (Figure 4) indicated that doses ≥ 400 mg are expected to achieve more that 50% of target engagement with the highest degree of HDAC inhibition (close to 90%) observed for the 600 and 800 mg doses. The derived t1/2 PD response was estimated at 6h post dose.

Figure 7. Concentration-Response curve for in Vivo HDAC inhibition in Human.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

<!-- image -->

The curve was derived from Emax and kD values obtained from PK/PD modeling. Cmax for each dose level was obtained with a non-compartmental analysis of PK data from Study 7341/EM-001 (FIM).

Overall, the data indicate that HDAC inhibition represents a suitable marker for resminostat PD activity

## 2.6.3. Discussion on clinical pharmacology

## Bioanalysis

Validation of analytical methods was performed before the implementation of ICH guideline M10 (January 21, 2023), therefore, differences between the bioanalytical method validation conducted were questioned. The impact on reliability of data was investigated by considering all validation parameters according to ICM M10. The main differences which could be found with regard to ICH M10 are reliability of data of specificity, matrix effect and reinjection reproducibility. The Applicant has sufficiently justified negligible effect on reliability of the data considering appropriate validation results of used bioanalytical methods.

## Influence of gastric modifiers

No investigation was performed to assess the effect of pH modulators such as antiacids, PPI or H2 receptor antagonists on the systemic exposure of resminostat. However, given the provided solubility data of resminostat mesilate at the relevant pH values, potential effects on absorption of resminostat mesilate resulting from pH modulations could be considered unlikely.

## Pharmacokinetic of metabolites

The Applicant clarified the elimination pathway of P21.M14-glucuronide as being the most abundant circulating metabolite (~15% of the systemic exposure) and its PD activity as well as that of its precursor. Excretion of P21.M14-gluc was shown via urine. However, the metabolite could also be subject to further glucuronidation (to form P24.M14-gluc) or deglucuronidation (to form M14-methylated) followed by excretion (urine, feces) or further metabolic transformation (to form M15). The plasma concentrations over time indicate that formation and disappearance of metabolite P21.M14-gluc was well balanced without peak concentrations. Based on the many options for metabolic pathways, accumulation of P21.M14-gluc or any of the other identified metabolites is considered unlikely. Regarding the PD activity, neither the metabolite P21.M14-gluc nor its precursor M14-methylated (for structures, see Figure 3) have been tested for their inhibitory activity towards human HDAC enzymes. However, based on the proposed structures of these compounds, the probability of a PD activity of P21.M14-gluc and its precursor is assessed as low.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Dose proportionality

Based on the PK data of the FIM study 7341/EM-001, the Applicant concluded that resminostat exhibited linear PKs in the dose range of [100 -800 mg]. Such claim is not endorsed. First, the study used an initial capsule formulation with significantly lower biopharmaceutical performances (40 to 50% on Cmax and AUC) compared to the commercial tablet formulation and very limited patients (n=2 to 3) were available in each dose level. Thus, no formal conclusion regarding dose proportionality could be drawn. Further, the observed systemic exposures of resminostat indicated rather a more than dose proportional increase. On one hand, after single dosing, the geometric mean Cmax and AUClast were 487 ng/mL and 1230 ng.h/mL at 100 mg versus 4970 ng/mL and 21100 ng.h/mL at 800 mg, respectively, indicating that AUClast increased by more than 2-fold the increase of dose in the claimed interval [100-800 mg]. On the other hand, the mean apparent CL/F clearly decreases with increasing dose levels: CL/F was 80.9 mL/h at 100 mg versus 37.7 mL/h at 800 mg. Overall, dose proportionality remains unclear. In this context, it should be highlighted that a unique dose level (i.e 600 mg) was investigated in the target population and using the formulation of interest. The Applicant was asked to comment the issue and amend the SmPC if applicable. The Applicant shared the uncertainty regarding the conclusion of dose proportionality and consequently the reference fo PK linearity of resminostat in the dose range of 100 to 800 mg was agreed to be removed from section 5.2 of the SmpC (dedicated \"Linearity/non-linearity\" paragraph and mentions in the absorption paragraph).

## Pharmacokinetic in the target population / Population PK analysis

Overall, the methodology used to develop the PPK model for resminostat is supported. The Applicant was asked to clarify the definition and implementation of some parameters (especially on the time-varying absorption model), to generate additional predictive performances plots and to further investigate covariates effects. As part of the Applicant's Day 120 responses, a new Pop-PK analysis was performed and the provided results (final model parameters estimates, covariate analysis, GOF plots, pcVPCs) are considered acceptable.

## Impaired renal function

The recommendation that no dose adjustment is needed in patients with moderate renal impairment was challenged. The Applicant provided the mean (SD) serum concentration-time profiles of resminostat at day 5 stratified by renal impairment groups, as well as a forest plot illustrating the effect of each renal impairment category on predicted model-based exposures metrics (Cmax, Cmin, AUCtau) at day 5 in comparison to the reference group of normal renal function. As per the provided data, the geometric mean ratios and 95% CI distribution of the log-transformed exposure parameters (AUCt, Cmax, Clast) were within the interval [0.81.25]. Hence, the recommendation that no dose adjustment is needed in patients with moderate renal impairment is considered agreed.

In section 5.2 of the SmPC, adequate wording was proposed to reflect that no PK data are available in the severe renal impaired group. Thus, the PK of resminostat in this subgroup of patients is considered unknown and consequently no dosing recommendation can be made. Given the major role of the renal elimination in the excretion of resminostat (up to 85%) and the absence of an adequate dosing regimen, a clear statement indicating that use of resminostat should be avoided in this patient population is to be added in sections 4.2 and 4.4 of the SmPC.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Impaired hepatic function

Resminostat has not been studied in patients with hepatic impairment. Therefore the SmPC was expected to explictely indicate in section 4.2, 4.4 and 5.2 that no dosing recommendation could be made in this population and that resminostat should be avoided in patients with hepatic impairment. (Please also refer to the clinical safety part).

## Gender

The Applicant was asked to further inform the potential effect of gender on the PKs of resminostat. The requested investigations / analyses were performed within the new Pop-PK analysis. The provided data (PopPK model-based simulations, pcVPCs) confirmed the higher systemic exposure on resminostat in females by approximately 30% (in terms of mean Cmax values 5725 versus 4384 ng/mL and mean AUCt 11257 versus 8725 ng.hr/mL) compared to male patients. However, the observed decreased resminostat's CL/F and V/F in women by 20% and 24% respectively, appears to be driven / explained by difference on body weight (BW) rather than gender. Indeed, a significant correlation between PK of resminostat and BW is identified and the distribution of weight indicate ~20% higher mean weight in males than in females (79.2 versus 65.3 kg). Overall, based on aggregated data, the Applicant's conclusion that no dose adpatation is warranted based on gender could be endorsed.

## Race / ethnicity

The Pop-PK dataset included a majority of Caucasian (94.7%, n=216 of 228) while only n= 12 (5.3%) Japanese patients (Phase 1 study YHI-1001). As such, the SmPC was proposed to be adequately amended to reflect the lack of PK data in others Asian ethnicities and in Black subjects.

## Body weight

The Applicant was asked to further investigate the effect of BW on the PKs of resminostat in obese (BW&gt;100 kg) and underweighted (&lt;50 kg) patients. The requested analyses were performed within the revised Pop-PK analysis submitted at Day 120. BW as a continuous covariate was tested and retained in the final Pop-PK model as a statistically significant covariate on both resminostat's CL/F and V/F. The analysis indicated a clear trend for increase of the systremic exposure on resminostat (Cmax, AUC) as patient's weight decreases and vice versa. For illustration, the mean resminostat exposure at steady state (AUCss) is expected to increase by approximately 49.5% (26114/17459 ng∙hr/mL) and by 37% (24024/17459 ng∙hr/mL) for patients with low BW of 30 and 40 kg respectively; and in contrast high BW of 110kg and 120 kg would result on a reduced AUCss in average by 21.3% (13751/17459 ng∙hr/mL) and 27.4% (12684/17459 ng∙hr/mL), respecetively after daily dose of 600 mg resminostat.

Even if it can be shared that a dose adjustment in underweighted patients &lt; 50 kg is not deemed necessary (at least at start of treatment), given the wide therapeutic range of the drug product and that a deteriorated safety profile is manageable by dose reduction as planned in the dosing regimen, the opportunity of a higher dose (i.e 800 mg) in obese patients with a BW &gt;100 kg, especially in view of the PFS results in this subgroup (hazard ratio of 1.486 in favour of placebo) suggesting a limited efficacy in obese patients, should have been further explored. This should be scrutinized based on the available clinical efficacy outcomes and considered in the overall assessment of efficacy/safety background of the 600 mg dose (Please refer to clinical efficacy part).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Elderly

A dedicated paragraph was proposed to be included in section 5.2 of the SmPC to inform the available PK data included in the analysed population PK dataset. As such, 30% (n=93/312) and 10% (n=31 /312) were in the [65-74 years] and [75-84 y] groups of age, respectively and only n=1 patient &gt;85 years was available. Lack of PK data in patient ≥85 years is clearly acknowledged.

## Pharmacokinetic interactions

Resminostat is mainly metabolysed by CYP2C8 and to a lesser extent by CYP1A2 and CYP2C19. No clinical study was conducted to assess resminostat potential interactions with CYP2C8, CYP1A2, and CYP2C19 inhibitors as victim drug, or resminostat induction by other drugs. These interactions were considered unlikely by the MAH due to the observed variety of metabolic pathways and the wide therapeutic range of resminostat reported during clinical trials.

Based on in vitro data [study 4SC-201-DDI-001], resminostat interactions as perpetrator drug is considered unlikely.

## 2.6.4. Conclusions on clinical pharmacology

The PK of resminostat was investigated using the non-compartmental and nonlinear-mixed effects modelling approaches. Data from 2 phase 1 studies in healthy volunteers, 4 phase 1/ 2 studies in patients with different oncologic indications and one pivotal phase 2 study in the claimed patients with advanced CTCL (MF and SS) were used for analyses. Overall, the PK properties of resminostat (200 mg tablet) are considered sufficiently characterized.

The provided Population-PK analyses show overall acceptable descriptive performance. An efficacy E-R analysis based on the clinical and PK data collected in the target patients with MF or SS indicate a clear correlation between the level of systemic exposure on resminostat (C-2h and AUC) and the clinical benefit in terms of PFS.

The proposed mechanism is considered understood; however, the effects are complex and multifaceted. The main PD biomarkers are specified and are supported by the non-clinical development.

## 2.6.5. Clinical efficacy

## 2.6.5.1. Dose response study

## Study 7341/EM-001

Open label, dose escalation trial of oral BYK408740 in patients with advanced malignancy: First-In-Man Study of a newly developed, oral HDAC inhibitor.

The 19 included patients were treated at five dose levels between 100 mg and 800 mg. Eighteen patients (18, 95%) were evaluable for the determination of MTD, and 18 were evaluable for response. Patients received 4SC-201 once a day on Days 1-5 of 14-day treatment cycles; the optional twice daily treatment regimen was not explored in this study.

The first DLT was observed in a patient treated at the highest dose level (DL IV, 800 mg); the event in question (grade 3 nausea and vomiting, with ALT elevation, hypokalemia and thrombocytopenia) was not strictly a DLT in accordance with the protocol definition, but was considered by the investigators and sponsor as a DLT due to the effect on the patient; three additional patients were added, with no further DLT reported. Thus the formal MTD for 4SC-201 administered on Days 1-5 of a 2-week cycle was not reached in this study. There is no exposure efficacy analysis to support the choice of the recommended dose.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## 2.6.5.2. Main study

## Title of Study

RESMAIN: A multicenter, double blind, randomized, placebo controlled, Phase II trial to evaluate resminostat for maintenance treatment of patients with advanced stage (Stage IIB - IVB) mycosis fungoides (MF) or Sézary syndrome (SS) that have achieved disease control with systemic therapy.

## Methods

Trial 4SC-201-6-2015 was a multi-center, double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled phase II trial to evaluate the efficacy and safety of maintenance treatment with resminostat. Adult patients with advanced stage (Stage IIB - IVB) MF or SS who had achieved disease control (CR, PR or SD) with previous systemic therapy were enrolled. The last systemic therapy had to be completed 2 - 12 weeks prior to randomization. Patients were randomized in a ratio of 1:1 to treatment with oral resminostat or matching placebo including stratification for current remission status (CR/PR versus SD) and disease stage (IIB/III/IVA1 versus IVA2/IVB) prior to last systemic therapy. All patients were treated until progression of disease or unacceptable toxicity. Patients on placebo had the option to roll-over to open-label treatment with resminostat.

## Study Participants

## Inclusion Criteria

-Patients with histologically confirmed MF (Stage IIB-IVB) or SS in an ongoing CR, PR or SD after at least one prior systemic therapy according to local standards (including but not limited to IFN-α, bexarotene, ECP, chemotherapy) or TSEB irradiation

The most recent systemic therapy had to be completed as planned or stopped due to unacceptable toxicity 2 - 12 weeks prior to randomization, i.e., patients should not be withdrawn from a treatment from which they derived benefit

- -Eastern Cooperative Oncology Group (ECOG) status score of 0 - 2
- -Adequate hematological, hepatic and renal function, as demonstrated by:
- a) hemoglobin ≥ 9.0 g/dL (International System [SI] of Units: 5.6 mmol/L)
- b) absolute neutrophil count (ANC) ≥ 1.0 × 109/L
- c) platelets ≥ 75 × 109/L
- d) ALT and aspartate aminotransferase (AST) ≤ 2 times upper limit of normal
- e) total bilirubin ≤ 2 mg/dL (SI units: 34.2 μmol/L) (unless known Gilbert syndrome)
- f) serum creatinine ≤ 1.5 mg/dL (SI units: 132 μmol/L)
- g) prothrombin time: International Normalized Ratio (INR) ≤ 2.3

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

-Adequate recovery from non-hematological toxicities, excluding alopecia, to ≤ common terminology criteria for adverse events (CTCAE) Grade 1

## Exclusion Criteria

1. Patients with progressive disease (PD)
2. Known central nervous system involvement
3. History and current cardiovascular complications, including unstable angina pectoris, uncontrolled hypertension, congestive heart failure (NYHA Class III or IV) related to primary cardiac disease, a condition requiring anti-arrhythmic therapy, ischemic or severe valvular heart disease, or a myocardial infarction within 6 months prior to randomization
4. Baseline QTc interval &gt; 500 milliseconds [NOTE: QTcF is relevant]
5. History of additional risk factors for Torsade de Pointes (e.g., heart failure, hypokalemia, family history of Long QT Syndrome)
6. Use of concomitant medications that are known to prolong the QT/QTc interval
7. Concurrent use of any other specific anti-tumour therapy including PUVA, chemotherapy, immunotherapy, hormonal therapy, radiation therapy, or experimental medications
8. Previous or concurrent cancer that was distinct in primary site or histology from CTCL, except curatively treated squamous-cell carcinoma of the skin stage 0 - 1 and curatively treated malignant melanoma stage 0 - 1A with a low risk of recurrence/metastasis as per assessment of the investigator, cervical carcinoma in situ, treated basal cell carcinoma, superficial bladder tumours (Ta, Tis and T1); any cancer curatively treated &gt; 3 years prior to randomization was allowed
9. Current evidence of any uncontrolled clinically significant internal, psychiatric or neurologic disease
10. Altered mental status precluding understanding of the informed consent process and/or completion of the necessary trial procedures
11. Pregnant or breast feeding women
12. History of allergic reactions attributed to compounds of similar chemical or biological composition to the trial medication
13. Active alcohol and/or drug abuse

14. Any other acute or chronic condition that, in the investigator's opinion, would have limited the patient's ability to complete or participate in this trial

15. Patients with tumoural stage MF, presenting at baseline only with tumours (thereof at least one tumour ≥ 1 cm in diameter), without current involvement of the skin by patches or plaques in conjunction with a modified severity weighted assessment tool (mSWAT) &gt; 0 and &lt; 20 (only applicable for Belgium)

16. Patients with a history of treatment with Vorinostat (only applicable for Japan)

## Treatments

Each patient received treatment with oral resminostat or a matching oral placebo. The treatment was administered in a double-blind fashion based on the treatment arm to which the patient had been randomized. Treatment was administered QD in repeated cycles of 5 days on therapy followed by a treatment-free period of 9 days (i.e., D1 - D5, q2w) until PD, unacceptable toxicity or patient withdrawal.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

After each EOT visit, patients who discontinued treatment due to progression were unblinded individually. Patients who had been randomized to treatment with placebo were offered to roll-over to open-label treatment with resminostat QD, D1 - D5, q2w for as long as they benefited from therapy in the opinion of the investigators, or until unacceptable toxicity or patient withdrawal.

Based on the obtained data on safety, tolerability, PK and pharmacodynamics, the dose of 600 mg QD (3 tablets of 200 mg each), D1 - D5, q2w was the recommended dose and dosing regimen for repeated treatment cycles with resminostat and thus selected for the present trial.

Missed doses should not be administered at a later time. In these cases, the doses should be skipped, and the next dose administered as scheduled.

## Objectives

The primary objective of the trial was to determine the superior efficacy of resminostat as a maintenance treatment based on progression free survival (PFS) in patients with advanced stage (Stage IIB - IVB) MF or SS who had achieved disease control (CR, PR or SD) with prior systemic therapy.

Secondary objectives of the trial were to:

- Determine efficacy of resminostat as a maintenance therapy based on secondary efficacy parameters (i.e., TTP, TTNT, PFS on the next treatment following study therapy (PFS2), PFS on the subsequent treatment (PFS3), ORR, DOR, and OS),

- Determine safety and tolerability of resminostat

- Determine changes in health-related quality of life (HrQoL) based on the key secondary endpoint (time to symptom worsening (TTSW): pruritus) and additional secondary QoL parameters (VAS for itching, FACT-G and Skindex-29 questionnaires).

The Applicant did not make use of the estimand framework. Nevertheless, the following estimand attributes are implied by the statistical analysis details that are provided as part of the SAP.

Table 7: Estimands for primary objective

| Population             | Patients according to eligibility criteria, with advanced stage (Stage IIB - IVB) MF or SS who had achieved disease control (CR, PR or SD) with prior systemic therapy                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
|------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Treatment condition<s> | Assignment to resminostat in the hypothetical scenario of no subsequent cancer therapy before progression, compared to assignment to placebo in the hypothetical scenario of no subsequent cancer therapy before progression. Treatment administered on Days 1 - 5 (600 mg p.o. QD) of a 14-day treatment cycle; 5 days of treatment followed by a 9-day treatment-free period. Treatment until PD or unacceptable toxicity. |
| Endpoint (variable)    | Progression Free Survival (PFS) defined as the time from date of randomization to first date the criteria for PD are met according to the GRS or death by any cause in the absence of documented PD. Only assessments within randomized                                                                                                                                                                                      |

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

|                                                                         | period are taken into account for derivation of PFS. The Randomized period is defined as the period from date of randomization to date blinded EOT data is collected.                                                                                                                                                                                        |
|-------------------------------------------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Population-level summary                                                | As population level summary, the Hazard Ratio of the two treatment groups (Resminostat/Placebo) together with the corresponding 95% CI using Cox's proportional hazards model stratified by stage of disease and remission status is calculated. Hazard Ratio (HR) from a Cox proportional hazards model stratified by randomisation stratification factors. |
| Intercurrent events and strategy to handle them                         | Intercurrent events and strategy to handle them                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Patients received non-study cancer treatment before disease progression | Hypothetical: censored in primary analysis at the date of last complete GRA before non-study cancer treatment or randomisation day (whichever comes later), thereby ignoring data after non-study cancer treatment and assuming censored at random.                                                                                                          |
| Death                                                                   | Composite: event, to reflect treatment failure                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |

The primary objective is a comparison of PFS between resminostat and placebo arms, in the hypothetical scenario that no non-study cancer treatment were received before progression. The intercurrent events (ICE) of initiation of non-study cancer before disease progression is handled using a hypothetical strategy, by censoring patients at the date of last complete GRA before its occurrence. In other words, data following nonstudy cancer treatment are ignored and the corresponding patients are assumed to be censored at random (CAR).

## Outcomes/endpoints

Primary endpoint:

Progression Free Survival (PFS) defined as the time from date of randomization to first date the criteria for PD are met according to the GRS or death by any cause in the absence of documented PD.

Secondary efficacy endpoints included:

- TTSW (pruritus; key secondary endpoint), defined as the time from date of randomization to first date the criteria for symptom (pruritus) worsening had been met.
- TTP, defined as the time from date of randomization to first date that criteria for PD (per the GRS) have been met.
- TTNT, defined as the time from date of randomization to first date that new treatment is received; for placebo patients, roll-over to open-label resminostat was considered as the next treatment
- PFS2 and PFS3, defined as the time from date of randomization to second/third objective disease progression, or death of any cause, whichever comes first (note: will only be available upon the final data analysis at the end of the clinical study)
- ORR (CR, PR), defined as the percent of patients within each treatment arm that achieve CR or PR relative to the number of patients belonging to the analysis population of interest

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

- DOR, defined as the time from date of CR or PR (whichever is first) until the criteria for PD per the GRS as specified in the protocol have been met.
- OS, defined as the time from the day of randomization to death from any cause.
- The analysis of HrQoL based on VAS for itching, FACT-G and Skindex-29 questionnaires, safety and the PK of resminostat and its metabolites.

## Sample size

## Final sample size determination from protocol version 7.0, 15 November 2021

The sample size calculation was based on the primary endpoint PFS. In order to calculate the number of required events, a PFS of 4.2 month in the control Arm (Arm B; placebo) was assumed. Based on a hazard ratio of 0.6, a median PFS of 7 months should be reached in the treatment Arm (Arm A; resminostat). An enrolment period of 24 months and 12 months FU was assumed. Based on the commonly used significance level, a two-sided α = 0.05 for the type 1 error was allocated as the significance level for the primary endpoint. Applying a power of 80% and a drop-out rate of 33% the total patient number needed was calculated with 200 patients to get 125 PFS events. For enrolment of 100 patients per treatment arm, a total of approximately 200 patients would be enrolled. The sample size calculation was performed with Pass 14 by NCSS, LLC.

No detailed results for the key secondary endpoint TTSW are available to be used as assumptions for the sample size calculation. However, the trial was powered to detect a hazard ratio of 0.6 for TTSW and such a hazard ratio was considered as clinically relevant in this context.

## Sample size determination from protocol version 6.0, 28 February 2019

The same assumptions as above were considered, with the exception of the drop-out rate which was assumed to be 30%. Accordingly, the total patient number needed was calculated to be 189 patients to get 125 PFS events. For enrolment of 95 patients per treatment arm, a total of approximately 190 patients were to be enrolled.

## Sample size determination in preceding protocol versions

The same assumptions as above were considered, with the exception of the drop-out rate which was assumed to be 10%. Accordingly, the total patient number needed was calculated to be 150 patients to get 125 PFS events.

## Randomisation and blinding (masking)

Eligible patients were randomized to treatment with resminostat or placebo at a ratio of 1:1 using IWRS. Randomization of patients was stratified by remission status (CR/PR versus SD, i.e., the best response achieved with the last systemic therapy) and by MF/SS disease stage immediately prior to the last systemic treatment which resulted in CR, PR or SD (stage IIB, III, IVA1 versus stage IVA2, IVB as assessed based on International Society for Cutaneous Lymphomas (ISCL)/European Organisation for Research and Treatment of Cancer (EORTC) revised staging system for MF/SS).

The trial was conducted as a double-blind trial.

Subsequently to stratification and randomization, the IWRS assigned a specific medication kit (per kit number) to each patient. The kit numbers were assigned to wallets, each containing one resminostat or placebo blister. This procedure was followed to ensure that investigators and patients (as well as Sponsor and CRO) did not know which treatment a patient received.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Each patient was unblinded individually at the time of progression to determine subsequent treatment or FU procedures. This EOT unblinding was performed by the investigator via the IWRS.

Emergency unblinding for a patient was also performed via the IWRS by the investigator or authorized team members with immediate access to the randomization code, if it was in the best interest of the patient. Once the randomization code was broken, the patient had to be withdrawn from treatment and entered FU for survival. The investigator had to inform the Medical Monitor about an emergency unblinding in writing within 24 hours. Unblinding of an individual patient did not affect the double-blind character of the trial. Unblinding of all ongoing patients was performed once the results of the main analysis (primary data analysis) were available.

Prior to unblinding of each patient, the patient and their Investigator were questioned for the assumed treatment arm of the patient. A total of 60.0% of both patients and Investigators guessed treatment with resminostat correctly, while 17.0% each assumed treatment with placebo (missing: 23.0% each). For placebo, 70.3% of the patients and 76.2% of the Investigators guessed the correct treatment arm, with 19.8% and 13.9%, respectively, assuming treatment with resminostat (missing: 9.9% each).

## Statistical methods

## Analysis populations

The main analysis of trial endpoints was based on the following analysis populations:

- ITT population: all randomized patients

- PP population: patients randomized, treated with at least one dose of trial medication and with no major protocol violations at the time of data review meeting for the main analysis
- Safety population: patients treated with at least one dose of trial medication

- TTSW (pruritus) population: all randomized patients with a VAS itching score of ≤ 7 at treatment start; patients without VAS itching score at baseline were excluded

- Roll-over population: patients from treatment arm B (placebo), who opted to receive open-label resminostat treatment after unblinding and were treated with at least one dose of resminostat

## Adjustment for multiplicity and interim analyses

The results for analysis of TTSW (pruritus) would only be considered confirmative, if the analysis of the primary endpoint showed a significant result (p-value ≤ 0.05). This hierarchical approach keeps the global significance level to 0.05 two-sided and required no further adjustment.

There was no planned adjustment for multiplicity for other secondary endpoints.

No interim analysis was planned for this study. Thus, the full significance (alpha) level of 0.05 was used for this main analysis and all analyses within the scope of the final analysis will be considered exploratory.

## Primary endpoint

PFS was defined as the time from date of randomisation to first date that criteria for PD have been met or death due to any cause in the absence of documented PD. The Global Response Score reported by the Investigator in the eCRF was used for the analysis. Global Response Scores may be based on less than four components in case of missing values/assessments. An event of progression was considered to be present as soon as PD was observed in at least one of the four components (regardless of the other (possibly unavailable/missing) assessments). Censoring for the PFS endpoint was assigned on the dates according to the rules in the table below.

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

|   ID | Situation                                                                            | Date of Progression or Censoring                                                                          | Outcome    |
|------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|------------|
|    1 | Baseline GRA missing/incomplete (for exceptions see 5.1.3.6)                         | Date of randomisation day                                                                                 | Censored   |
|    2 | Patient received non- study cancer treatment before disease progression              | Date of last complete GRA before non- study cancer treatment or randomisation day (whichever comes later) | Censored   |
|    3 | No progression or death (at least 1 complete post- baseline GRA)                     | Date of last complete GRA                                                                                 | Censored   |
|    4 | Death or progression directly after at least two consecutive incomplete/missing GRAs | Date of last complete GRA                                                                                 | Censored   |
|    5 | Progression or death documented between or on scheduled visits                       | Date of PD or date of death (whichever comes first)                                                       | Progressed |
|    6 | No complete post- baseline GRA                                                       | Date of randomisation day                                                                                 | Censored   |

Note: the date of last global response assessment (GRA) is defined as the date of the last GRA = all four components of the GRA measured with censoring date being the last of the four components. If all 4 components were assessed before Day 43 and no later complete GRA assessment or PD exists, these assessments should be used for censoring. A component that is not involved is not considered to be missing.

A two-sided stratified log-rank test, taking strata (stage of disease, remission status) into account, was used to compare the median PFS between the two treatment arms.

Kaplan-Meier PFS curves (product-limit estimates) were presented by treatment arm including the number of patients at risk at selected time points.

The Hazard Ratio (HR) of the two treatment groups (Resminostat/Placebo) was calculated together with the corresponding 95% CI using Cox's proportional hazards model stratified by stage of disease and remission status.

## Sensitivity analyses

1. A sensitivity analysis was conducted where patients who leave the study due to toxicity or start of new treatment (and no PD was observed prior to that) are not censored but considered as having PD on the date of discontinuation.
2. A sensitivity analysis was conducted where the censoring rule ID 4 was changed to:
3. A sensitivity analysis was conducted where PFS was not only considered to be present until the last Global Response Score without PD that is based on all four components but also until the last

| Death or progression directly ater one or more consecutive incomplete/missing GRAs   | Date of last complete GRA   | Censored   |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------|------------|

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Intermediate Response Assessment without PD that is based on the remaining two components (mSWAT and Flow Cytometry) for visits were no CT is to be conducted.

4. A sensitivity analysis was conducted where the response assessment rules for skin from protocol version 3.0 was applied. PD in skin was derived separately as increase of ≥ 25% from baseline in mSWAT. The exception for patients who entered the study with a CR (mSWAT of 0) or with a very good PR to prior therapy (mSWAT below 16) and for whom any increase in mSWAT to less than 20 and without new tumours (reflecting still stage IA skin disease) should not be considered progressive disease, but SD in skin, is not applicable for this sensitivity analysis. In consequence, any increase in mSWAT is considered as PD for patients with mSWAT of 0 at baseline.
5. A sensitivity analysis recommended by the DSMB was conducted for PFS where censoring rule ID 2 was changed to:

| 2   | Patient received non- study cancer treatment before disease progression   | Date of study treatment discontinuation   | Progressed   |
|-----|---------------------------------------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------|--------------|

## Key secondary endpoint

The same analysis method as that described for PFS was used for analysis of TTSW (pruritus worsening).

Censoring for the TTSW (pruritus) endpoint was assigned on the date of the last on trial assessment of symptom (pruritus) worsening if criteria for symptom (pruritus) worsening had not been met.

## Other secondary endpoints

The same method as that described for PFS were to be used for analysis of TTP, TTNT, PFS2, and PFS3.

TTP: The censoring rules for PFS were applied with the exception, that only disease-related deaths were considered in rule 3, 4 and 5.

TTNT: If a patient does not start a new (next) treatment after discontinuing from study drug, then TTNT was censored on the date of last contact. For placebo patients, rollover to Resminostat was considered as the next treatment.

PFS2/PFS3: A comparison is planned at the final analysis between the patients who were randomized to Resminostat and the patients who were randomized to Placebo and did not roll over to Resminostat. Patients who were randomized to Placebo and roll over to Resminotat are planned to be excluded to compare Resminostat patients with those who were never treated with Resminostat. Censoring rules for analysis are given below:

|   ID | Situation                                                           | Date of Progression or Censoring                                                                                                                                                     | Outcome    |
|------|---------------------------------------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|------------|
|    1 | No progression during first or second subsequent treatment or death | End date of last subsequent CTCL therapy or radiotherapy or date of last complete GRA (whichever comes later). If therapy is ongoing, date of study discontinuation is used instead. | Censored   |
|    2 | PD during first or second subsequent treatment                      | Date of PD                                                                                                                                                                           | Progressed |
|    3 | Death during any randomized or subsequent treatment                 | Date of death                                                                                                                                                                        | Progressed |

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

ORR: Only Global Response Scores that are based on all four components were considered for the evaluation of the ORR. If no post-baseline Global Response Score based on all components exists, the overall response cannot be derived for the respective patient. A comparison of the ORR (CR or PR) in each of the treatment arms was made using a Cochran-Mantel-Haenszel (CMH) test taking strata used for randomisation (stage of disease, remission status) into account. The ORR (CR or PR) together with corresponding 95% CI, odds ratio and corresponding 95% Clopper-Pearson exact CI and p-value for the CMH test were presented.

## DOR

For patients who achieve a PR or CR during the trial, DOR was calculated as the number of months from the date measurement criteria are first met for a PR or CR (whichever is recorded first) as determined by global response score assessment (based on four components) until the first date that PD is determined by global response score assessment. Patients with baseline remission status CR are excluded from this analysis. DOR was calculated as (date of first PD - date of PR/CR) + 1. Patients who achieve PR or CR but for whom subsequent PD is not observed during the trial are censored at the time when the patient is last known to be non-progressing. Last known to be non-progressing is the date of last global response score assessment meeting the criteria for CR or PR or SD not limited to global response score assessments based on all four components. DOR was analysed for the patients in the ITT population who had CR/PR during the randomised treatment period.

## OS

The number and percentage of patients that died, together with reason for death was summarised for the ITT population. A two-sided stratified log-rank test, taking strata (stage of disease, remission status) into account, was used to compare the median OS between the two treatment arms.

For patients who are known to be still alive at the time of trial analysis or who are lost to FU, survival time was censored at the last recorded date that the patient is known to be alive (i.e. the date of last attended visit or the date of the follow-up phone call). Not only data from randomised period but also rollover and follow-up data were taken into account for derivation of overall survival.

## HrQoL

A linear MMRM approach was applied to analyse change from baseline over time of HrQoL data. The MMRM included treatment arm, baseline, time point (continuous), time point (continuous) by treatment interaction and time point (continuous) by baseline interaction as fixed effects.

## Recruitment

Patients were enrolled at 55 trial sites in 12 countries, including 11 European countries (i.e. Austria, Belgium, France, Germany, Greece, Italy, The Netherlands, Poland, Spain, Switzerland and United Kingdom (UK)) and Japan.

First patient enrolled: 14-Dec-2016

DCO primary analyis: 10-Mar-2023

## Conduct of the study

## Protocol Amendments

The original protocol was issued on 07-Apr-2016. There were 7 amendments to the protocol. At Japanese sites, the trial was conducted according to protocol versions 4.0 through 8.0 but required additional protocol appendices due to country-specific regulation.

## Protocol Deviations

In the resminostat arm, a total of 10 patients (10.0%) were excluded from the PP population. In 5 patients, this was due to determination of PD not fulfilling the PD criteria defined in the study protocol (and thus the blind broken). Another 5 patients were excluded for not being able to be assessed for the ECG parameter of QTc (leading to early discontinuation), missing first 2 post-baseline GRS assessments, violation of eligibility criteria, missing GRS before PD and wrong subject medication given (in 1 patient each).

In the placebo arm, a total of 17 patients (16.8%) were excluded from the PP population due to determination of PD not fulfilling the PD criteria defined in the study protocol (and thus the blind broken) in 13 patients, missing GRS before PD in 3 patients and wrong subject medication given in 1 patient.

## Numbers analysed

All randomized patients were included in the ITT population (n = 201) which was the primary population for assessment of efficacy. Selected efficacy parameters were also analyzed for the PP population (n = 174) comprising all randomized patients without protocol violations. Analysis of the key secondary endpoint TTSW pruritus was based on the TTSW population and included all patients with a VAS itching score of ≤ 7 at baseline (n = 154).

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

## Outcomes and estimation

## - Primary endpoint (PFS)

Analysis of PFS in the ITT population was based on 47 events (in 47.0% of patients) observed on resminostat and 70 events (in 69.3% of patients) on placebo. 53 and 31 patients, respectively, were censored in each treatment arm.

## Overall Response Rate

Patients with a baseline remission status of CR (n = 25) were excluded; the ORR rate was evaluated in patients with baseline remission status of PR or SD only (n = 176), with response evaluated by the GRS based on all four categories.

On resminostat (n = 86), 3 patients (3.5%) achieved CR and 12 patients (14.0%) achieved PR, leading to an ORR of 17.4% [95% CI: 10.104, 27.131]. On placebo (n = 90), none of the patients achieved CR and 9 patients (10.0%) achieved PR, leading to an ORR of 10.0% [4.676, 18.136].

## Duration of Response

The DOR was evaluated in patients with response to trial treatment (n = 24). With 19.4 months [6.5, not evaluable (NE)] on resminostat and 20.3 months [7.3, NE] on placebo, median DOR was similar in both treatment arms.

## Overall Survival

The results for OS at the final analysis (data cut off: 08 August 2024) were similar to those at the main analysis and showed no significant difference between the treatment arms (p-value 0.940, HR 0.983 (0.606, 1.590)). The respective Kaplan-Meier curves are depicted in Figure 5.

Figure 8 Overall Survival (ITT Population, Final Analysis)

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

<!-- image -->

Analyses of PFS on 2 nd (PFS2) and on 3 rd line therapy (PFS3), for the ITT population were performed at the final analysis time point (data cut off: 08 August 2024). The results obtained by a stratified log-rank test and a Cox-PH model do not indicate a statistically significant difference between treatment arms for PFS2 (pvalue 0.156, HR 0.758 (0.516, 1.113)) and PFS3 (p-value 0.921, HR 1.021 (0.674, 1.547)).

## Key Secondary Endpoint: Time to Symptom Worsening (Pruritus)

## Secondary Quality of Life Endpoints

Visual Analogue Scale for Itching

The Visual Analogue Scale (VAS) of 0 -10 points for itching (higher value = worse) was collected every 14 days, i.e., at baseline and at Day 1 of each new treatment cycle. At baseline, analysis of VAS itching was based on 91 patients on resminostat and 92 patients on placebo and comprised mean scores of 3.20 points (median: 1.80; range: 0 - 10) and 3.20 points (median: 2.40; range: 0 - 10), respectively.

## Functional Assessment of Cancer - General (FACT-G) Questionnaire

The FACT-G score (total score of 0 - 108 points including the domains physical, social, emotional and functional well-being; lower value = worse) was collected every 28 days, i.e., at baseline and at Day 1 of every other treatment cycle. At baseline, analysis of FACT-G was based on 92 patients on resminostat and 93 patients on placebo.

On resminostat, FACT-G scores were assessed from baseline up to Day 1905. At baseline, a mean total score of 81.4 points (median: 84.8; range: 26 - 103) was reported. Over time, mean total scores were in the range of 65.0 - 93.0 points. A decrease (worsening) was observed predominantly in the \"physical well-being\" domain, from a mean of 23.3 points (median: 25.0; range: 7 - 28) at baseline to the lowest mean of 15.7 points (median: 18.0; range: 4 - 25) reported at Day 1037 (n = 7).

On placebo, FACT-G scores were assessed from baseline up to Day 1345. At baseline, a mean total score of 80.9 points (median: 82.0; range: 41 - 107) was reported. Over time, mean total scores were in the range of 79.4 - 100.7 points. In particular, the \"physical wellbeing\" score did not decrease as observed for resminostat, but remained stable.

Changes from baseline in FACT-G total scores were analyzed based on the ITT population using a linear MMRM. 83 patients on resminostat and 89 patients on placebo were included in the analysis. Timepoints/visits with less than 5 observations for at least one treatment arm were not included in the analysis. The observed changes from baseline over time (i.e. Day 29 to Day 981) for resminostat versus placebo were statistically significant in favor of placebo (p = 0.041).

(ITT Population)

## Skindex-29 Questionnaire

The Skindex-29 score (total score of 0 - 100 points including the domains symptoms, emotional and functioning scale; higher value = worse) was collected every 28 days, i.e., at baseline and at Day 1 of every other treatment cycle. At baseline, analysis of Skindex-29 was based on 92 patients on resminostat and 91 patients on placebo and comprised mean total scores of 35.46 points (median: 34.53; range: 0.7 - 100) and 33.41 points (median 30.10; range: 0 - 80.4), respectively. In both treatment arms, the number of patients with available Skindex-29 scores decreased over time. On resminostat, Skindex-29 scores were assessed from baseline up to Day 1,905, with mean total scores in the range of 15.63 - 48.24 points. On placebo, Skindex-29 scores were assessed from baseline up to Day 1,345, with mean total scores in the range of 19.27 - 37.49 points Changes from baseline in Skindex-29 total scores were analyzed based on the ITT population using a linear MMRM. 82 patients on resminostat and 88 patients on placebo were included in the analysis. The LS mean difference (resminostat-placebo) was 0.04 (-3.55, 3.63) at day 85.

## Exploratory Analyses

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

Requirement of Anti-Itching Treatment

The number of patients who did not have any anti-itching treatment at baseline but started at least once such a treatment during the randomized period was the same in both treatment arms (n = 21 each). In these patients, the use of anti-itching medication was similar in both treatment arms and comprised predominantly corticosteroids and antihistamines for systemic use.

The number of patients with at least one anti-itching treatment at baseline who at least once did not receive any anti-itching treatment for ≥ 7 consecutive days during the randomized period was similar on resminostat (n = 16) and on placebo (n = 14).

The number of patients with at least one anti-itching treatment at baseline who continued this treatment during the randomized period was similar on resminostat (n = 15) and on placebo (n = 12). Dose changes were reported for 8 patients (8.0%) on resminostat and 5 patients (5.0%) on placebo and comprised, in all patients, the addition of a new anti-itching treatment while the existing one was stable during the randomized period.

## Effect of Treatment on mSWAT Scores over Time

Changes from baseline in mSWAT scores were analyzed based on the ITT population using a linear MMRM. 90 patients on resminostat and 96 patients on placebo were included in the analysis. Timepoints/visits with less than 5 observations for at least one treatment arm were not included in the analysis. The observed changes from baseline over time (i.e., Day 29 to Day 1,093) for resminostat versus placebo were statistically significant in favor of resminostat (p = 0.0199).

Time to Development or Worsening of Skin Tumours

The time to development or worsening of skin tumours was analyzed as TTTE based on mSWAT assessments for the ITT population as well as the subgroups ITT-TTTE1 (no skin tumour present at baseline) and ITTTTTE2 (skin tumour present at baseline).

To account for the heterogeneity of the CTCL population, PFS was analyzed in additional subgroups, including demographic parameters (age, sex, race) as well as baseline disease parameters known as unfavorable prognostic factors such as histologically confirmed lymph node (N3) and/or visceral involvement (stage IVA2 and IVB) or presence of LCT. The forest plot of PFS for all subgroups is presented.

A post-hoc subgroup analysis of the primary endpoint PFS was performed based on the ITT population for each of the disease stages IIB (ITT-DSIIB), III (ITT-DSIII) and IV (ITT-DSIV) and for the disease stages III and IV (ITT-DSIII/IV) combined.

Evaluation of FACT-G Subscales and a Modified FACT-G Total Score

In addition to evaluation of changes from baseline in FACT-G total scores using an MMRM approach, the 4 subscales of the FACT-G questionnaire were analyzed separately and subject to the same MMRM model. The difference in changes from baseline was statistically significant in favor of placebo in both the functional wellbeing and the physical well-being subscale scores (Table 27). Furthermore, a modified FACT-G total score was determined, with the physical well-being question \"I have nausea\" set to \"not at all\" for all patients to test the impact of nausea (an AE frequently observed in patients on resminostat). The modified score was also subject to MMRM analysis. The treatment effect using the modified FACT total score was -2.9463 [95%

<div style=\"page-break-after: always\"></div>

CI: -6.4123, 0.5197], i.e., still in favor of placebo (p = 0.0954), but smaller than the effect of the original FACT-G total score (-3.736 [-7.310, -0.162]) (Table 27).
